2026-09-20 19:44: Task 1/95 [product] "Make connecting independently running agents the
    primary purpose of Solvr, with Rooms first and one unified Post model for shared
    knowledge". Direction task: encoded the north star as EXECUTABLE invariants rather
    than prose (golden rules forbid new doc files) and recorded today's verified baseline.

    NEW: backend/internal/api/router_product_direction_test.go (252 lines) — two
    integration tests pinning the two product rules the API can enforce today:
      - TestProductDirection_AgentsConnectWithoutHumanAccount: planner agent
        self-registers (public POST /v1/agents/register, no Authorization header), then
        creates and owns a real room with its own agent key (owner_id absent = no human
        owner) while holding an 'owner' room_members row so the ownerless room stays
        manageable; an independently registered executor handshakes with the shared room
        token and gets its OWN solvr_rt_ per-agent credential; both join, exchange
        messages with server-stamped author_id, and read the one shared timeline; the
        exchange is readable logged out. No human account, no claiming, no hand-made API
        key, no plugin, no MCP server anywhere in the journey.
      - TestProductDirection_NoHardCodedParticipantLimit: 9 independently registered
        agents handshake/join/post in ONE room; asserts 9 membership rows, 9 presence
        rows and all 9 messages on the timeline. 9 clears both the two-agent shape of the
        planner/executor example and any eight-agent ceiling.

    [REAL] ralph.sh completion rules replaced. The old "tasks that need the owner degrade" clause was
        too broad: it let an iteration pass a task when the environment could not run the checks.
        Now: a SKIPPED test is not a passed test; environment-blocked (no database, Docker down) is
        STUCK — journal the exact error, leave passes=false, stop; the owner-only degrade applies ONLY
        when every automated check already ran green; acceptance tasks spanning unbuilt features stay
        open. Workflow step 4 requires reading test output and naming the database it ran against.
    [REAL] ralph.sh stall guard added: STALL_LIMIT (default 2) iterations with no ledger progress ends
        the batch with "RALPH BLOCKED" (exit 2), and ralph-continuous.sh stops instead of retrying all
        night. Verified with a stub engine: ralph.sh exit 2, supervisor exit 1.
    [REAL] router_product_direction_test.go cleanup bug fixed. setupRoomTestServer's cleanup ends in
        pool.Close(); deferred funcs run before t.Cleanup funcs, so both t.Cleanup delete blocks were
        executing against a CLOSED pool and their errors were swallowed by //nolint:errcheck. The
        helper's own sweep only deletes agents matching 'agent_roomtest_%', so agent_pd_% rows leaked.
        Replaced with `defer pdCleanup(t, pool)` registered AFTER `defer cleanup()` (LIFO puts it
        before the close) and cleanup failures are now logged, not swallowed.
        MEASURED both ways on a clean database: original code left 11 agent_pd_% rows behind; fixed
        code leaves 0.
    [REAL] Docker Desktop was dead, not just stopped: com.docker.backend.log said
        "backend crashed: opening tray: starting electron: unmarshaling start request: unexpected EOF"
        then "stopping local engine linux/virtualization-framework" / "monitor exited: exit status 150".
        The VM never started because the backend aborted launching its Electron tray. Installed 4.48.0
        (Oct 2025 binary) on macOS 26.5. Reinstalled to 4.91.0 via `brew install --cask docker-desktop`
        (the owner ran it — the cask needs sudo to link into /usr/local/bin). Daemon now up, server 29.8.0.
    [REAL] TestProductDirection_AgentsConnectWithoutHumanAccount PASS (0.31s) and
        TestProductDirection_NoHardCodedParticipantLimit PASS (0.89s), run with -count=1 against an
        isolated pgvector/pgvector:pg17 container (solvr-test-pg, host port 5455, database solvr_test)

      - Logo -> "/". Primary nav is a labelled landmark <nav aria-label="Primary"> holding

        link. Skill (/skill), API reference (/api-docs), MCP (/mcp) and Guides (/docs/guides)
        live beneath it and are absent from the DOM until it is opened.

        second filled CTA competing with it). LOG IN stays a quiet text link. Signed in: the
        account menu (UserMenu) replaces LOG IN; Connect stays.

    sharp geometry, and generous spacing". Frontend-only; no backend file touched.

    The supplied 2026-09-20 homepage screenshot is NOT in this checkout (no image anywhere in
    the repo tree), so the references used were frontend/app/globals.css and the pages served
    by a running dev server. A preservation task with no executable teeth is prose, so the
    design system is now a CONTRACT that fails loudly when something drifts.

    NEW: frontend/design-system.test.tsx (441 lines), 45 tests, ALL PASS. It parses
    app/globals.css, resolves Tailwind's own palette out of node_modules/tailwindcss/theme.css
    (no hard-coded hexes anywhere), converts OKLCH -> linear sRGB and computes real WCAG
    ratios, then scans every non-test .ts/.tsx under app/, components/, lib/ and hooks/.
    What it pins:

2026-09-20 23:45: Task 5/95 [functional] "Show a real planner/executor collaboration as the
    primary homepage proof". Backend + frontend. The proof is now SERVED, not hard-coded: a new
    public endpoint reads the real room and the homepage renders whatever it answers.

    STEP 1 — read the real room first (read-only, no writes to production):
      curl https://api.solvr.dev/v1/rooms/tictactoe-human-vs-computer-20260920 -> 200, is_private
      false, message_count 9, last_active_at 2026-09-20T19:49:32Z, two agent authors
      (raphael_tictactoe_planner, raphael_tictactoe_executor). The five beats the task names map
      to sequence numbers 2 (PLANNER DIRECTIVE v1), 4 (IMPLEMENTATION PLAN), 5 (PLAN APPROVED),
      6 (IMPLEMENTATION COMPLETE) and 9 (PLANNER CLOSED — final review).

    NEW API: GET /v1/homepage/example — public, no auth, always 200.
      backend/internal/api/handlers/homepage_example.go (408 lines)
      backend/internal/api/router_homepage.go (30 lines, mountHomepageRoutes; router.go +3 lines
        only — it is already 1169 and over the CI limit, so the wiring lives in its own file)
      The API decides EVERYTHING the visitor sees, per the smart-API rule:
        - which five messages are the beats. Roles come from the transcript, NOT from agent
          names: the agent that opens the room is the planner, the next distinct agent to speak
          is the executor. Beats are then (a) the planner's last message before the executor
          arrives, (b) the executor's last message of its first turn, (c) the planner's next
          message, (d) the executor's next message, (e) the planner's LAST message. Human and
          system messages are never beats. Fewer than five resolvable turns -> fallback.
        - the excerpt: cut at 240 runes on a word boundary, "…" appended, is_excerpt=true and
          excerpt_note "Excerpt — the original message is N characters". A message that fits is
          returned whole and is NOT called an excerpt (excerpt_note absent).
        - the link back: message_url = /rooms/<slug>#message-<sequence_num> per beat.
        - completed vs live: "completed" unless agent_presence reports live agents in that room
          RIGHT NOW (TTL-bounded rows, the API's own independent confirmation). Only then is it
          labelled LIVE COLLABORATION. The completed summary states the age of the last message
          (API-formatted: "moments ago" / "N hours ago" / "N days ago") so the browser never
          decides what "recent" means.
        - the fallback: room private, deleted, missing, unreadable, or too short -> kind
          "illustrative", label ILLUSTRATIVE WORKFLOW, NO room object, NO room_url, NO
          participants, NO authors, NO message links, NO excerpts — only the five beats described
          generically plus connect_url "/connect?preset=planner-executor".
      Room slug is configurable via HOMEPAGE_EXAMPLE_ROOM_SLUG (default
      tictactoe-human-vs-computer-20260920). No env file was touched.

    NEW FRONTEND: components/collaboration-example.tsx (154 lines) + hooks/use-collaboration-
      example.ts, rendered on app/page.tsx directly under the hero (the proof sits with the claim
      it proves). lib/api-base.ts gains getCollaborationExample(); lib/api-types.ts gains the
      response types. The component renders API strings and decides nothing — a test reads its
      source and fails if it ever contains a slug, a transcript, a beat label, "Excerpt", the
      completed/live wording, .slice(0,N) or substring(.
    components/rooms/message-bubble.tsx: each message now carries id="message-<sequence_num>"
      and scroll-mt-24. Without that anchor every "link each excerpt to its original message"
      would open the room and land nowhere.

2026-09-21 00:35: Task 6/95 [functional] "Make the index a rich live overview of Solvr activity
    while keeping navigation simple". Backend + frontend. The index is now SERVED, not composed

    NEW API: GET /v1/homepage/overview — public, no auth, always 200. Eight sections in the exact
    order step 1 names, plus GET /v1/homepage/activity?offset=&limit= as the Load more behind the
    stream.
      backend/internal/db/homepage_overview.go        (397 lines) HomepageRepository
      backend/internal/api/handlers/homepage_overview.go (725) sections + handler
      backend/internal/api/handlers/homepage_activity.go (346) stream + editorial previews
      backend/internal/api/router_homepage.go         (+13 lines; router.go untouched — it is
                                                       already 1172 and over the CI limit)
    The API decides EVERYTHING the visitor sees, per the smart-API rule:
      - which numbers are worth showing, and for each one the WINDOW it was measured over plus a
        DEFINITION of what it counts (step 5's "windows and definitions are visible" is a field on
        every metric, not a caption someone might forget).
      - the sparkline: 24 hourly buckets, zero-filled, with each bar's height already expressed as
        a CSS percentage ("50.0%"). The first draft had the browser doing `normalized * 100`; the
        guard test caught it and the arithmetic moved into the API where it belongs.
      - the activity stream: six items, each excerpt cut at 200 runes on a word boundary and
        labelled "Excerpt — the original message is N characters", each relative time worded by the
        API ("moments ago" / "3 hours ago" / "2 days ago"), each item linked to /rooms/<slug> and
        to #message-<sequence_num>. The repo is asked for limit+1 rows so the API — not the
        browser — decides whether there is more, and hands back the next URL.
      - the editorial previews (step 4): an EXPLICIT ALLOW-LIST, HOMEPAGE_PREVIEW_ROOM_SLUGS,
        defaulting to the supplied coding example (tictactoe-human-vs-computer-20260920), capped at
        3, rendered in the configured order. There is NO ranking by message_count anywhere in the
        path — the one thing step 4 forbids is the one thing the code cannot do. Each preview
        carries purpose (the room's description), participants (distinct authors, oldest first),
        a real exchange (the most recent two consecutive messages by DIFFERENT authors — a
        monologue is not an exchange and returns empty), last activity, and selected_reason so the
        page says WHY a room is there and nobody reads it as a leaderboard.
      - API usage: only MEASURED call volumes (agent/human searches 7d, room messages delivered
        24h, agents registered). There is no request-log table in this schema, so nothing here is
        an estimate dressed up as a metric.
      - search: total/agent/unique/zero-result-rate/avg-latency, the API formatting the percentage
        ("31%") and the latency ("3 ms") so the browser does no arithmetic, plus three compact
        tables — trending, recent and the type-filter breakdown.
      - PRIVACY RULE ON RECENT QUERIES, and it is stated on the page: a term only appears once it
        has been searched MORE THAN ONCE in the window (recentQueryMinOccurrences = 2). A one-off
        query can be traced to one visitor and can contain anything they typed.
      - all-time community totals, each stamped "all time"; an unread counter renders the API's own
        "—", never an invented 0.
      - reusable Posts: public posts carrying >= 1 contribution (answer/approach/progress note),
        newest activity first, routed to /problems|/questions|/ideas per type, with the rule that
        selected them printed on the page. draft/pending_review/rejected are excluded.
      - the closing block: /connect + "Connect agents now".

    PUBLIC-ONLY BY CONSTRUCTION. Every repo query carries is_private = FALSE AND deleted_at IS NULL
    on rooms and visibility = 'public' on posts; system messages are never activity.

WHAT SHIPPED (backend first, then the page — API smart, client dumb)

  NEW backend/internal/db/homepage_rooms.go (339 lines)
    - RoomStatsWindow / RoomStatsWindows / DefaultRoomStatsWindow /
      RoomStatsWindowByValue: the 24h / 7d / 30d vocabulary, default 24h, an
      unknown value REJECTED (ok=false) rather than silently coerced.
    - RoomPresenceStats (now, never windowed) vs RoomWindowStats (windowed),
      deliberately separate types so the two can never be confused in a call.
    - GetRoomPulse(ctx, window): one round trip for all eleven scalars plus the
      series. Live figures use liveRoomPredicate (public + not deleted + NOT
      EXPIRED); historical figures use publicRoomPredicate, so a retained
      expired room keeps its transcript but leaves the "now" numbers — step 6.
    - Verified vs unverified identity: agent_presence carries only agent_name
      (migration 000074 has no identity column), so presence alone is a CLAIM.
      A presence row counts as VERIFIED when room_agent_tokens holds a live
      per-agent token for (room_id, agent_name) — i.e. the agent completed the
      handshake with its own Solvr key (migration 000079). Unverified =
      total - verified. Same idea for messages: author_id is stamped only when
      a per-agent room token authenticated the post (rooms_messages.go:116-119),
      so author_id IS NULL = posted with the shared token = unverified.
    - messageSeries: hourly buckets for 24h, daily for 7d/30d, zero-filled,
      oldest first, system messages excluded.
    - RoomActivationEventType = "room.activated" and
      RoomEventRepository.RecordActivation(ctx, roomID): records the two-way
      exchange milestone the FIRST time a room holds messages from >= 2 distinct
      non-system, non-deleted authors. One INSERT ... SELECT ... WHERE NOT
      EXISTS: idempotent, never a running count.
  backend/internal/db/homepage_overview.go: old RoomPulse/HourlyCount/
    GetRoomPulse/messagesPerHour removed (superseded). 397 -> 294 lines.

  NEW backend/internal/api/handlers/homepage_rooms.go (312 lines)
    - OverviewRooms split into presence_metrics (window "now", presence:true)
      and metrics (windowed), plus scope_label "Public room activity",
      scope_note (states the public-room count and that private rooms are never
      counted), window_options/selected_window, and the sparkline.
    - The six metrics task 7 names: agents_online_now,
      rooms_with_agents_online_now | rooms_with_conversation, agent_messages,
      human_messages, rooms_with_two_way_exchanges.
    - NO CONVERSION RATE ANYWHERE. A test marshals the section's own words and
      asserts "conversion", "rate" and "%" never appear; activation conversion
      stays in internal analytics, per step 1.
    - The exchange milestone degrades honestly: never instrumented ->
      unavailable:true + display "—" + a qualifier saying so, never 0.
      Instrumented after the window opened -> the count plus "measured since
      <date>; earlier history is unavailable, not zero". Step 5.
    - parseRoomStatsWindow: absent/unknown -> 24h. Safe because the answer
      always states the window it measured, so a fallback cannot mislabel.
    - GetRooms serves GET /v1/homepage/rooms, Cache-Control public max-age=30.
  backend/internal/api/handlers/homepage_overview.go: rooms/sparkline types and
    builders moved out; OverviewMetric gained presence / unavailable /
    qualifier; GetOverview now honours ?window=. 725 -> 626 lines.
  backend/internal/api/router_homepage.go: GET /v1/homepage/rooms mounted.
  backend/internal/api/handlers/rooms_messages.go + router_rooms.go: the
    milestone is now RECORDED. RoomMessagesHandler took an eventRepo (the one
    router_rooms.go already builds) and both PostMessage and PostHumanMessage
    call recordActivationMilestone — best effort and logged, so a milestone
    that cannot be written is a statistic briefly short, never a message that
    failed to post. Emitting the milestone is what gives step 5's "within the
    window" any meaning; task 61 still owns the activation FUNNEL measurement.

  [REAL] A TEST I WROTE FAILED THE FIRST FULL-SUITE RUN, AND WHY IT NOW DOES NOT.
      TestGetRoomPulse_HistoricalMetricsFollowTheSelectedWindow passed alone and
      failed under `go test ./...`. Cause, measured not guessed: go test runs
      packages in parallel, internal/api posts room messages throughout, and my
      tests asserted EXACT before/after deltas on GLOBAL aggregates. The delta
      was never a stable measurement. Fixed by replacing the deltas with an
      independently written CENSUS (takeRoomCensus: same definitions, written
      with subqueries and make_interval instead of joins and string casts) taken
      in the same breath as the repository call. Ambient rows land in BOTH
      numbers, so a wrong predicate — counting a private room, a deleted
      message, a system notice, an expired room's presence — still shows up as a
      disagreement, while concurrent traffic cannot manufacture one. Where an
      absolute claim was still needed the tests use a floor on rows they own.
      The API-level privacy test was hardened the same way: the deterministic
      proof is that neither the private room's content nor its slug appears
      anywhere in the raw body, and the EXCLUSION is pinned one layer down.

WHAT THE TASK ASKED FOR, AND WHAT IT NOW DOES.
  The stream used to be "the last message from each public room": author name,
  excerpt, relative time. It is now a feed of WORK.

  [REAL] ONE TIMELINE, TWO KINDS OF ENTRY. New repository read
      HomepageRepository.ListPublicRoomFeed (backend/internal/db/
      homepage_activity.go) UNIONs public-room MESSAGES with public-room TYPED
      EVENTS (room_events), newest first. The superseded ListPublicRoomActivity
      and db.PublicRoomActivity were deleted — v0, no back-compat.
  [REAL] TRANSPORT IS INVISIBLE BY CONSTRUCTION. Typed events pass an
      ALLOW-LIST (db.PublicFeedEventTypes: CLAIM, RELEASE, BUILDING, PLAN,
      DIRECTIVE, EVIDENCE, REVIEW, BLOCKED, PR, MERGED, DONE), not a noise
      blocklist. A heartbeat, a token operation, a presence renewal or task 7's
      internal `room.activated` milestone can never appear, because only listed
      work types can. A type invented tomorrow stays out until somebody decides
      it is work worth publishing.
  [REAL] THE ACTION LABEL IS THE POSTER'S OWN. A message labelled in its
      metadata ({"type":"plan"}, "kind"/"action" also read) becomes "Shared a
      plan" / "Posted a directive" / "Submitted evidence" / "Left a review" /
      "Reported progress" / "Handed the work over"; a typed event becomes its
      own sentence ("Claimed render the board"; "Started building" when the
      agent named no issue). Anything unlabelled, unparseable or outside the
      vocabulary reads "Posted a message" and carries action_stated=false. The
      API never guesses an intent out of message text.
  [REAL] BURSTS GROUP. Consecutive entries from one room collapse into one
      block with a count_label and a burst_note, so a busy room cannot push
      every other room off the page. Only CONSECUTIVE entries group: a room
      that returns later in the stream is its own block, because merging it
      would reorder the timeline.
  [REAL] EVERY ENTRY LINKS TO ITSELF. link_url + link_label: the exact anchor
      (/rooms/<slug>#message-<seq>, "Open the original message") when the entry
      has a sequence_num, the room otherwise. MEASURED GAP, LEFT FOR THE TASK
      THAT OWNS THE ROOM PAGE: frontend/components/rooms/ renders messages with
      #message-<seq> anchors (message-bubble.tsx) but does NOT render typed
      events at all, so there is no #event-<id> to land on. Rather than ship a
      link promising a precision it does not have, an event entry addresses the
      room and SAYS SO in its own label. Task 33+ owns the room page.
  [REAL] REFRESH NEVER MOVES THE READER. The payload carries cursor +
      refresh_url; GET /v1/homepage/activity?since=<cursor> answers with
      has_new / new_count / new_label ("3 new updates", "50+ new updates" at
      the cap, never an exact-looking capped number). The count is taken BEFORE
      the page is read so an entry landing between the two reads is reported
      rather than missed, and it is capped (CountPublicRoomFeedSince, cap 50)
      so a reader returning after a week cannot turn one control into an
      expensive count. The browser polls on an interval while the page is
      visible and shows the API's label as a button; the list changes only when
      the reader clicks it.
  [REAL] NO OUTCOME IS READ OUT OF VOLUME. outcome_note ships with the section
      ("Every label here is the poster's own... Solvr never reads a result out
      of how much a room talks") and a test marshals the whole payload of a
      24-message room and asserts the words solved/completed/succeeded/
      finished/shipped appear nowhere in it.

A REAL PRIVACY DEFECT FOUND AND FIXED ON THE WAY.
  [REAL] backend/internal/api/handlers/rooms_messages.go:237 stores a human
      room comment as agent_name = "human:<user uuid>" ("deterministic, not
      displayed" — but frontend/components/rooms/message-bubble.tsx DOES
      display it). The old activity query selected m.agent_name straight out,

      it fail with `+human:8bd56fd6-4993-4913-9b3b-2b657606d8eb`, then fixed

      page still renders the raw value; that is its own page's bug and its own
      task's call, and it is written down here rather than fixed silently.

2026-09-21 02:45 — TASK 9 "Show what people and agents search for on the
  homepage" [analytics]. DONE, passes=true.

FIRST, WHY NOT TASK 6 — RE-VERIFIED THIS ITERATION, NOT TAKEN ON TRUST:
    - `ls frontend/app/connect` -> `agent/` only. There is still no /connect
      page; next.config.mjs redirects /connect -> /connect/agent. Task 16 owns
      it.
    - `grep -rn "connect" backend/internal/api/router*.go` and `ls
      backend/internal/api/handlers/ | grep -i connect` -> NOTHING. There is no
      GET /v1/connect and no room-instruction endpoint. Task 25 owns the
      "API-owned instruction contract" that task 6 step 4 requires.
  Task 6 asks for a homepage panel carrying "the same task, visibility, preset
  and copy controls used by /connect" and sharing that contract. Building it
  means doing tasks 16+17+25 inside task 6. Per "ACCEPTANCE TASKS ARE NOT
  CHECKLISTS", TASK 6 REMAINS OPEN and I took the first buildable task: 9.

THE ONE IDEA THIS TASK TURNS ON: TWO ELIGIBILITY RULES, NEVER ONE.
  Search is the closest a public page comes to publishing a log, so counting
  and quoting are decided separately and the page says which is which.
    - COUNT eligibility decides what is MEASURED: every search in the window
      minus known automated monitoring. Searches whose eligibility is unknown
      are counted.
    - TEXT eligibility decides what may be QUOTED: the search must PROVABLY
      have run over public content AND the text must pass db.PublicQueryText.
  [REAL] A REAL LEAK THIS CLOSES, FOUND BY READING THE CODE, NOT GUESSED.
      GET /v1/search is FAMILY-SCOPED: handlers/search.go:203 sets
      opts.ViewerHuman = callerHumanID(r), and db/visibility.go
      searchVisibilityClause turns a non-empty value into
      "(visibility='public' OR owner_human_id = <that human's>)". So a
      signed-in human — AND an agent claimed by one, via agent.HumanID — can
      match posts that are NOT public. The old homepage published trending and
      recent terms straight out of search_queries with no scope predicate at
      all, so the term that found somebody's private post was publishable.
      search_queries had no column recording this, which is why it could not
      simply be filtered: the information was never stored.

WHAT I BUILT.
  [REAL] MIGRATION 000082 adds search_queries.public_scope BOOLEAN, NULLABLE
      ON PURPOSE. TRUE = the search ran public-only. FALSE = family-scoped,
      could have reached protected content. NULL = recorded before this
      existed, eligibility genuinely UNKNOWN. Plus a partial index
      (searched_at DESC) WHERE public_scope. Applied to the local dev database
      by hand (there is no schema_migrations table here); solvr_test was then
      recreated from it as TEMPLATE.
  [REAL] handlers/search.go now records `publicScope := opts.ViewerHuman == ""`
      on every search — the same expression the visibility clause branches on,
      so the record cannot drift from the query that was actually run.
  [REAL] db/search_query_policy.go — PublicQueryText(query, publicScope) is the
      single gate, returning a NAMED reason: protected_scope, unknown_scope,
      credential, personal, moderated, unpublishable. It is one-directional:
      a term is published only when positively known to be safe.
      - CREDENTIALS are matched by the SHAPE OF A VALUE, never the topic. This
        is the difference between a knowledge base and a word filter: "how to
        rotate an api key", "jwt token expired error" and "reset postgres
        password" all STAY (there is a test that says so), while sk-…,
        solvr_sk_…, AKIA…, ghp_…, xox[baprs]-…, a three-segment JWT, a PEM
        header, NAME=<value> for password/secret/api_key/…, "Bearer <opaque>",
        credentials inside a URL, and any unbroken 40+ char run are withheld.
      - The NAME=<value> rule anchors the name at a word start, so
        DATABASE_PASSWORD=hunter2 matches and "bypass=true" does not. I wrote
        that test first and watched the naive \b version fail it.
      - PERSONAL: emails, +-prefixed or grouped phone numbers, 11+ digit runs.
        "error 500 on deploy", "postgres 17 upgrade" and "http 429 rate limit"
        stay — also a test.
      - MODERATED: a short built-in floor, EXTENDED (never replaced) by
        SEARCH_QUERY_BLOCKLIST, matched at word starts so "pornography" is
        caught and "xxhash" is not. No .env was touched; the variable is read
        with a default.
  [REAL] db/homepage_search.go — HomepageRepository.GetSearchPulse(window,
      limit). Totals, the per-searcher-type breakdown, the separated monitoring
      figure, the unknown-scope count, the bucketed series, the top terms and
      the recent terms. The term reads require `public_scope` IN SQL, so a
      family-scoped row is never even read out of the table, and PublicQueryText
      then decides on the text. Because that policy cannot be expressed in SQL
      the reads over-fetch and report WithheldTerms, so a short list is never
      mistaken for a quiet week.
  [REAL] KNOWN MONITORING IS AN ALLOW-LIST OF NAMES, NOT A BOT HEURISTIC.
      db.KnownMonitoringAgents lists UptimeRobot, Pingdom, BetterStack,
      StatusCake, Site24x7, Datadog, Checkly, New Relic, GoogleHC, kube-probe,
      ELB-HealthChecker and friends. Guessing at "looks automated" would delete
      real agent traffic: every Solvr SDK and most agents send
      "Go-http-client/1.1", which is exactly the activity this page exists to
      show. There is a test named for that (AGenericClientIsNotAssumedToBeAMonitor).
      COALESCE(user_agent,'') matters: NULL ILIKE ANY is NULL, and without it
      every credential-less caller would drop out of the count entirely.
  [REAL] ANONYMOUS IS ITS OWN ANSWER. The breakdown is agent / human /
      anonymous taken from the RECORDED request, and the three partition the
      total exactly — which is precisely why anonymous cannot be folded into
      human: the total would stop adding up. searcherTypeLabel falls back to
      "Anonymous" for anything unrecorded or unrecognised; the one direction it
      may never fail in is calling an unidentified caller a person.
  [REAL] THE COUNT-ONLY AGGREGATE IS LABELLED ON THE NUMBER ITSELF, not left
      for a reader to work out: the SEARCHES tile carries "N of these were
      recorded before Solvr recorded whether a search could reach protected
      content; they are counted here and their terms are never published".
  [REAL] THE CHART SHIPS WITH ITS OWN TABLE. GET /v1/homepage/search returns
      series.sparkline AND series.rows built from ONE series, so they cannot
      disagree; the component renders both, the table as a real <table> with
      scope="col"/scope="row" and a caption. A test asserts row i equals
      point i, and that the buckets add up to the total printed above them.
  [REAL] EVERY PUBLISHED TERM LINKS TO /posts?q=<url-escaped term>. One query
      parameter: no type=, no legacy problem/idea filter, nothing needing an
      account. The API builds and escapes it ("c++ & rust <interop>" ->
      /posts?q=c%2B%2B+%26+rust+%3Cinterop%3E, tested).

A DEAD LINK WITH A 200 STATUS, FOUND AND FIXED.
  [REAL] /posts redirects to /feed (next.config.mjs), and app/feed/page.tsx ->
      FeedPageClient started from defaultFilters and NEVER READ the URL. So
      /posts?q=<term> resolved 200 and then ignored the term — every published
      search link would have landed on an unfiltered feed. FeedPageClient now
      seeds searchQuery from ?q= (trimmed), which FeedList already routes into
      useSearch. No type filter is read: the link is a query, not a category.
      useSearchParams needs a boundary under static rendering, so the page wraps
      the client in <Suspense>; `npm run build` confirms /feed is still
      prerendered static (○), 52/52 pages generated, compiled clean.

UAT: PRODUCTION MIGRATION. 000082 has not been applied to production. Until it
    is, the column does not exist and GET /v1/homepage/search will error. It is
    additive and safe (ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS + a partial index), and prod has
    no schema_migrations table, so it goes through the admin query route:
      ALTER TABLE search_queries ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS public_scope BOOLEAN;
      CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_search_queries_public_scope
        ON search_queries (searched_at DESC) WHERE public_scope;

THE ACTUAL DEFECT THIS TASK FIXED (measured on the local database, not reasoned)
    The old "Everything Solvr holds" section read its numbers from
    StatsRepository.GetAllStats, and two of them were WRONG in the direction
    that flatters:
      HUMANS  = `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM users`             -- counts DELETED users
      AGENTS  = `... FROM agents WHERE status='active'`  -- no deleted_at filter
      POSTS   = every non-deleted public post            -- counts DRAFTS
    On the local database that is 12 humans instead of 10, and 22 posts instead
    of 13. There was no public-rooms total at all, and the "What agents call"
    section carried a metric "AGENTS REGISTERED ... Agents that hold an active
    Solvr key" — a REGISTRATION sitting among CALL VOLUMES, which is precisely
    the conflation this task is named after.

  NEW backend/internal/db/homepage_totals.go (82 lines)
    - AllTimeTotals{PublicRooms, PublishedPosts, RegisteredAgents,
      RegisteredHumans} and HomepageRepository.GetAllTimeTotals: one round trip,
      four subqueries, an error on failure rather than a zeroed struct.
    - publishedPostStatuses is a DENYLIST (draft, pending_review, rejected) on
      purpose: posts.status carries twelve lifecycle values and every other one
      describes a readable post, so a future status counts without anyone
      remembering to edit this file.
    - PublicRooms deliberately INCLUDES expired rooms. Expiry archives a room,
      it does not erase it — dropping them would shrink Solvr every time a room
      went quiet. The windowed figures in homepage_rooms.go still exclude them
      from the LIVE numbers; the two rules now sit beside each other, commented.

  CHANGED backend/internal/api/handlers/homepage_overview.go (498 -> 543)
    - buildOverviewCommunity(totals, stats): heading "All time", seven metrics.
      public_rooms / published_posts / registered_agents / registered_humans
      come from the new read; total_contributions / problems_solved /
      crystallized_posts still come from GetAllStats. The two reads FAIL
      INDEPENDENTLY: whichever one failed marks only its own metrics
      Unavailable + "—". The old code set the display to "—" but left
      Unavailable false, so a failed read was indistinguishable from a
      measurement; it now says so.

      the next iteration does not "helpfully" add a trend.

      cleanup() now says why it may never be a t.Cleanup.

2026-09-21 — TASK 11 (analytics): Keep website traffic and growth intelligence
private while publishing product activity
================================================================================

TASK 6 WAS SKIPPED, ON PURPOSE, AND IT STAYS passes=false.
    Task 6 wants "Connect agents now" on the index to open an inline panel
    carrying "the same task, visibility, preset, and copy controls used by
    /connect", with "the full /connect page remain[ing] directly linkable" and
    "both surfaces us[ing] the same API-owned instruction contract".
    [REAL] There is no /connect page: frontend/app/connect holds only
    connect/agent/page.tsx, so the hero's href="/connect" is a 404 today.
    [REAL] There is no instruction contract either: no route in
    backend/internal/api/router*.go serves room instructions, and no handler
    file matches instruct|connect|prompt.
    Both are other tasks' work — task 16 builds /connect, 17 and 18 build the
    planner/executor prompts, 25 serves the versioned machine-readable
    instructions. Building task 6 means building those first, so by the
    "acceptance tasks are not checklists" rule I left it open and took the
    first task that is buildable now: task 11.

WHAT TASK 11 IS. Solvr measures two different things and only one of them is
    public. PRODUCT ACTIVITY (rooms, participation, eligible search, API call
    volume, labeled product totals) is the product working in the open.
    WEBSITE TRAFFIC AND GROWTH INTELLIGENCE (visitors, sessions, page views,
    DAU/WAU/MAU, bounce, acquisition, referrers, geography, conversion,
    retention, campaigns, growth targets) is operator analytics. The danger is
    not a traffic chart on the homepage — it is an internal analytics field
    being serialized and merely not drawn, where curl still finds it.

WHAT I BUILT.
  backend/internal/api/handlers/public_overview_allowlist.go (NEW, 282 lines)
    - PublicOverviewSections: the 9 top-level sections the public payload may
      carry, each with what it is for.
    - PublicOverviewMetrics: the 21 metric keys that may be published, each
      filed under one of five categories (room activity, participation in
      public rooms, eligible search activity, aggregate API usage, labeled
      product/community totals).
    - privateAnalyticsConcepts: 16 excluded ideas as ORDERED regexes, so a
      failure names the concept it found rather than only that something
      leaked. PrivateAnalyticsTermIn (text) and PrivateAnalyticsTermInKey
      (snake_case field names read as words) are the two entry points.
    - publicOverviewAuthoredTextKeys / PublicOverviewAuthoredText: the fields
      whose text the API itself writes. Participant content (excerpts, room
      names, post titles, search terms) is deliberately NOT vocabulary-checked
      — what may be quoted from it is decided by the publishing rules in
      homepage_search.go and homepage_activity.go. A public room is allowed to
      be about traffic analysis.
    - enforcePublicOverviewMetrics / enforcePublicRoomsMetrics /
      enforcePublicSearchMetrics: the allowlist is applied ON THE WAY OUT, in
      the API, immediately before serialization. A metric nobody named is
      withheld and logged at ERROR — it is never serialized and left for the
      browser not to draw.
  Wired into the three public handlers (5 lines in homepage_overview.go, and
    GetRooms/GetSearch now build a named section and enforce before writing).

================================================================================
2026-09-21 — TASK 6: LET VISITORS COPY THE FIRST CONNECTION PROMPT DIRECTLY
              FROM THE HOMEPAGE
================================================================================

WHAT THE TASK NEEDED THAT DID NOT EXIST YET. Nothing on the index could copy a
prompt, /connect had no page at all (next.config redirected it to
/connect/agent, which pushes a logged-out visitor to /login), and there was no
API-owned instruction contract for two surfaces to share. So this task built
the contract first and gave both surfaces the same component.

BACKEND — GET /v1/connect, THE START-FLOW CONTRACT (public, no auth).
  backend/internal/api/handlers/connect.go          (329 lines)
  backend/internal/api/handlers/connect_prompts.go  (182 lines)
  backend/internal/api/router_connect.go            (29 lines, mounted from
      router.go beside mountHomepageRoutes; router.go is already over the
      file-size limit so the route lives in its own file)

  The API decides EVERYTHING the panel shows: heading, intro, the task field's
  label/placeholder/note/bound, the two option groups and their question, which
  preset and which visibility is selected, what each choice MEANS in words, the
  copy control's label and its Copied label, the sentence that names the agent
  the prompt must be pasted into, the two initial copy/paste steps, the example
  link, the note that copying alone connects nothing — and the prompt text
  itself, character for character.
    ?task=       optional free text, bounded at 2000 runes (400 above it)
    ?preset=     plan-and-build (default) | collaborate  -> 400 on anything else
    ?visibility= public (default) | private              -> 400 on anything else
  An unknown value is refused rather than guessed: the API never picks a room
  shape somebody did not ask for.

  The prompt is written in Go, not in the browser, because the endpoints it
  names, the identity it demands and the visibility it creates are product
  decisions. It uses absolute production URLs (the agent reading it is not in
  the browser that copied it), tells the agent to REUSE its own Solvr identity
  or self-register, create the room it will own, take its own per-agent room
  token through handshake, join, post the task, and hand back the real room URL
  plus a complete prompt for the second agent — and to report a failed call as
  the exact error, never as a fabricated room link. It contains no "$" and no
  "${" anywhere, so nothing in it can read as a shell or template expansion,
  and the slug placeholder is the unmistakable ROOM_SLUG.

  The example link only points at the showcase room while that room really is
  public and readable; missing, deleted, private or unreadable degrades to the
  public rooms list rather than to a dead link. The slug is the same
  HOMEPAGE_EXAMPLE_ROOM_SLUG the homepage example uses, so the two surfaces
  can never point at different collaborations.

FRONTEND — ONE PANEL, TWO SURFACES.
  frontend/components/connect/connect-panel.tsx  (255 lines) — the shared panel
  frontend/hooks/use-connect-start.ts            (56)  — holds only what the
      visitor typed or chose; a choice not yet made is NOT sent, so the API's
      defaults are the only defaults anywhere
  frontend/lib/connect-types.ts                  (93)  + re-export from
      lib/api-types.ts, and api.getConnectStart() in lib/api-base.ts
  frontend/app/connect/page.tsx                  (29)  — the full page
  frontend/components/hero-section.tsx           — Connect agents now is now a
      control that opens the panel INLINE under the proposition (aria-expanded
      / aria-controls), not a link that leaves the index
  frontend/components/header.tsx                 — a compact CONNECT action in
      the mobile bar, outside the menu
  frontend/next.config.mjs                       — the /connect -> /connect/agent
      redirect is GONE (that placeholder was the reason /connect demanded a login)

  The panel renders API strings only. It writes no prompt, no endpoint, no
  visibility rule; it types into a field, sends the choice back, and copies the
  characters it was given.

2026-09-21 — TASK 12 (analytics): Show aggregate API usage publicly without
exposing website traffic or request details
================================================================================

WHAT IT NEEDED: an API-activity section on the homepage showing successful API
calls, agent API calls and distinct room/knowledge operations over the shared
24h / 7d / 30d window with a compact series — MEASURED AT THE API BOUNDARY, not
estimated from message totals. Before this the "What agents call" section was
built from search_queries and messages: exactly the derivation step 6 forbids.

WHAT WAS BUILT

  BOUNDARY MEASUREMENT (new)
    backend/migrations/000083_create_api_request_events.{up,down}.sql
      One row per confirmed application request: request_id, route_template,
      method, operation, operation_family, operation_kind, actor_type,
      status_class, occurred_at. NO body, query string, slug, id, IP, user
      agent or credential — a row can identify nobody. UNIQUE partial index on
      request_id (WHERE NOT NULL) is what makes counting idempotent.
    backend/internal/api/middleware/api_usage.go (+ _test.go)
      Records after the handler returns, when chi knows the ROUTE PATTERN.
      Excluded and therefore never usage: /health*, /v1/health*, /admin*,
      /metrics, /v1/homepage* (the page reading its own numbers), robots/
      sitemap/static/_next, any */stream (transport) and */heartbeat, OPTIONS
      preflights, and any request that matched no route (a scanner hitting
      /wp-login.php is not an application call). Kind: GET/HEAD = poll,
      */search* = search, everything else = write. Actor from the credential
      PRESENTED: solvr_sk_ = human, other solvr_ (agent key, solvr_rm_,
      solvr_rt_) = agent, a three-part token = human, nothing = anonymous —
      an unauthenticated caller is never reported as a person.
      Canonical OPERATION names collapse route adapters: POST
      /v1/rooms/{slug}/messages and POST /r/{slug}/message are both
      room.message.send, so "distinct operations" counts distinct WORK.
    backend/internal/db/api_usage.go
      APIUsageRepository.RecordRequests (multi-row INSERT ... ON CONFLICT
      (request_id) WHERE request_id IS NOT NULL DO NOTHING) and
      AsyncAPIUsageRecorder: bounded queue (1024), one writer, batches of 64
      or a 1s tick. A full queue DROPS rows and logs; measuring never slows
      down the product it measures.
    backend/internal/db/homepage_api_usage.go
      GetAPIUsagePulse(window): successful calls, the actor breakdown, the
      poll / write+search split, COUNT(DISTINCT operation) over the room and
      knowledge families, MIN(occurred_at) as instrumented-since, and a
      gap-free bucket series.

  PUBLISHING
    backend/internal/api/handlers/homepage_api_usage.go (+ _test.go)
      buildOverviewAPIUsage(pulse) and GET /v1/homepage/api-usage behind the
      same selector as rooms and search. Five metrics: api_calls_succeeded,
      agent_api_calls, room_knowledge_operations, passive_poll_calls,
      write_and_search_calls (the last two partition the first exactly).
      The headline figure carries the qualifier that keeps request volume
      apart from deduplicated domain writes: "one incoming request counts once
      even when a proxy retried it or the domain deduplicated the write it
      asked for". With nothing recorded every figure reads "—" and is flagged
      unavailable, never 0; when recording started inside the window each
      figure says "Measured since <date>; earlier history is unavailable, not
      zero" and the section repeats it once in start_note.
    handlers/public_overview_allowlist.go — the three DERIVED keys
      (agent_searches_7d, human_searches_7d, room_messages_24h) are GONE from
      the allowlist and the payload; the five measured keys replace them under
      CategoryAggregateAPIUsage. start_note added to the authored-text keys so
      the excluded-vocabulary walk covers it.
    handlers/homepage_overview.go — the overview now reads GetAPIUsagePulse and
      no longer reads the 7-day search summary for this section at all.
    api/router.go — apimiddleware.APIUsage(db.NewAsyncAPIUsageRecorder(pool))
      when a pool exists; api/router_homepage.go mounts the new endpoint.

  FRONTEND (dumb terminal)
    components/homepage/api-usage-section.tsx is now a client section with the
      window selector, MetricGrid, the sparkline + its accessible table, the
      start note and the scope note. It renders API strings only: no
      arithmetic, no thresholds, no wording of its own, and it prints the
      API's "—" rather than deciding a missing measurement is a zero.
    lib/homepage-types.ts (+ APIHomepageAPIUsageResponse), lib/api-base.ts
      (getHomepageApiUsage), live-overview.tsx, overview-fixture.ts, and the
      ApiUsageSection tests moved out of overview-sections.test.tsx into
      components/homepage/api-usage-section.test.tsx.

VERIFICATION

2026-09-21 — TASK 13 (operations): Restrict operator analytics and growth
             reports to explicit administrative access
================================================================================

WHAT THE TASK IS ABOUT

  Solvr measures two different things. PRODUCT ACTIVITY is published (tasks
  5-12 built that, and handlers/public_overview_allowlist.go decides exactly
  what reaches a visitor). OPERATOR ANALYTICS is the other one: traffic,
  acquisition, retention, cost, raw diagnostics and growth planning — the
  reporting Solvr does about ITSELF. That belongs to the people who run the
  product, and to nobody else.

  Two dangers, both server-side:
    1. ACCESS. A credential the product issues — a human account, an agent API
       key, a user key, a room owner, a room-scoped token — is a credential for
       USING Solvr, not for reading Solvr's own numbers. Hiding a link is not
       access control; the rule has to hold for somebody typing the URL.
    2. CACHING. An operator report a shared cache may store can be handed to
       the next visitor who asks for the same URL. A public overview request
       must never be able to hit an entry an operator's request created.

WHAT WAS THERE ALREADY, AND WHAT WAS NOT

  [REAL] Every /admin handler already checked X-Admin-API-Key itself, in THREE
      hand-rolled copies (admin.go checkAdminAuth, search_analytics.go
      checkSearchAnalyticsAuth, incidents_admin.go checkIncidentAdminAuth),
      each comparing the key with != (not constant time).
  [REAL] NOT ONE of them set a cache header. A GET /admin/search-analytics/
      trending answered 200 with no Cache-Control at all, which a CDN or proxy
      is free to cache heuristically. That was the real gap this task closes.
  [REAL] No GA4 or Search Console integration exists in the backend at all
      (grep over internal/ and cmd/): there are no credentials to leak there.
      The frontend carries the COLLECTION side only — the gtag measurement id
      in app/layout.tsx and sendGAEvent calls in lib/analytics.ts. A
      measurement id runs in every visitor's browser by design; it is not a
      credential, and a test that flagged it would only teach the suite to lie.

WHAT WAS BUILT

  backend/internal/api/handlers/operator_analytics.go (NEW, 205 lines)
    - OperatorAccessHeader — the one credential that opens a report.
    - OperatorReports — the registry of surfaces that REPORT (search-analytics
      trending + summary, email history, deleted users, deleted agents, and
      /admin/query, which can read anything the product stores). The router and
      the contract tests read the same list.
    - RequireOperatorAccess — one server-validated gate. Constant-time compare
      (crypto/subtle). An UNCONFIGURED server is CLOSED: an empty ADMIN_API_KEY
      answers 503, it never compares equal to an empty header.
    - ApplyOperatorReportCachePolicy — "no-store, private, max-age=0,
      must-revalidate" + "Pragma: no-cache" + "Vary: X-Admin-API-Key,
      Authorization, Cookie", applied to EVERY answer including refusals.
    - OperatorCredentialTermIn / ...TermInKey — the vocabulary for GA and
      Search Console material: reporting properties, the Data API, Search
      Console, service accounts, client/API secrets, application default
      credentials, a pasted PEM key, analytics exports, BigQuery. Deliberately
      NOT the collection tag, and deliberately not bare "private key" or
      "refresh token": Solvr issues private keys to agents and refresh tokens
      to people, and a rule that flagged those is a rule nobody could keep.

  backend/internal/api/router.go
    - All 13 /admin routes now go through r.With(operatorOnly). The handlers
      keep their own key check as the second lock.

  frontend/lib/operator-analytics-privacy.test.ts (NEW, 160 lines)
    - The browser collects and never reads: no reporting credential, property
      or export in shipped source; no operator key (X-Admin-API-Key /
      ADMIN_API_KEY) anywhere a browser could download it; no call to an
      operator report path; lib/analytics.ts sends events and never fetches;
      no analytics/traffic export served from public/.

VERIFICATION

  [TEST] backend handlers — 8 new unit tests, ALL RUN AND PASSED: the registry
      describes every report; the gate refuses 13 credential shapes (human
      bearer, agent key in both headers, user key, room token, owner+token,
      empty header, wrong key, a prefix of the real key, a key differing in the
      first byte, in the last byte, and one with trailing whitespace) and never
      reaches the handler; the operator's own key passes; an unconfigured
      server refuses everyone with 503; refusals are readable JSON errors;
      every answer — served, refused, wrongly keyed — is no-store, private,
      never public, and Vary-keyed; and the GA/GSC vocabulary names reporting
      material while leaving the product's own private keys, refresh tokens,
      api_key and the word "property" alone.

  [TEST] backend router — 6 new integration tests over REAL HTTP against a
      LOCAL isolated database (postgres://localhost:5435/solvr_test, a template
      clone of the local dev database migrated to 83, dropped afterwards).
      Production was never touched.
        - Every registered report refused to all 8 caller kinds, using REAL
          credentials issued by the running API: an ordinary human JWT, an
          agent API key (both header shapes), a room owner, the room token that
          room handed back, owner+token together, a guessed operator key, and
          anonymous.
        - THE WHOLE MUX WALKED: chi.Walk enumerates the registered routes, and
          every /admin route is probed with every caller — 104 subtests
          (13 routes x 8 callers), all refused 401/403, all carrying the
          private policy. The test reads the router rather than a hand list, so
          an /admin route added later is covered the day it is added.
        - Operator reporting still WORKS for the operator (summary, trending,
          email history all 200 with the private policy) — keeping traffic off
          the public index did not cost the ability to read it.
        - An unconfigured server answers 503, not the report.
        - The public overview can never share a cache entry with a report:
          different paths, the report is never stored, and the overview answers
          the operator byte-identically (same field-name set, no operator
          material) so an entry an operator's own visit created is safe.
        - No public statistics surface carries GA or Search Console material
          (all 12 endpoints in publicPrivacyEndpoints).
        - CORS does not admit X-Admin-API-Key, so no page on an allowed origin
          can send the operator credential from a browser at all.

NOTES / BOUNDARIES

2026-09-22 — TASK 19 "Give the executor a single pasteable prompt that joins the
planner's existing room" — COMPLETED (passes=true)
================================================================================

WHAT THE TASK ASKED FOR. A room-specific executor prompt, served from
GET /v1/rooms/{slug}/connect, that a second agent can paste to join a
planner-owned room with its OWN identity — naming the real room, the executor
role, the expected planner identity, the initial task, the real production
endpoints it must call, and NEVER containing the planner's credentials.

THE IMPLEMENTATION. The executor prompt (executorPromptText in
connect_prompts.go) is room-specific: it uses the real slug, not ROOM_SLUG,
in every URL. It names the expected planner as the first message author,
carries the initial task verbatim from that message, and instructs the
executor to use its OWN identity — reuse an existing agent key or self-register,
run its own handshake for its own per-agent room token (solvr_rt_), join presence,
read the room, post its plan, and follow planner feedback.

The prompt is served by RoomConnectHandler.GetRoomConnect (rooms_connect.go),
which resolves the real room, enforces the private-room 403 for anonymous callers
(same read policy as room detail), looks up the first message to infer the planner
identity and task, and returns the envelope {instruction_version, room_slug,
room_url, private, task, expected_planner_identity, executor_prompt,
first_message_id, first_message_url}. The first message is fetched via
MessageRepository.GetFirstMessage (room_messages.go), and the endpoint is wired
into the router alongside the other room read routes with the shared readGuard
applying the same access-control policy.

The prompt never contains the planner's API key or room token — it only names
endpoints and placeholders. It never uses shell/template substitution ($/${/). It
never directs the executor to impersonate the planner — it explicitly says "Use
your OWN identity for every call — this token is yours alone."

TESTS — ALL RAN, ALL GREEN, NONE SKIPPED

  [TEST] Backend handler unit tests (no DB), pure builders:
    go test ./internal/api/handlers/ -run TestRoomsConnect -count=1 -v
    Result: 7 tests PASS:
      - TestRoomsConnect_ExecutorPromptNamesActualRoom: names real room URL,
        executor role, expected planner identity, initial task, real endpoints
        (handshake/join/message/messages with real slug), "use your OWN
        identity", never invents, no "$", no `${`, no backticks, no localhost,

WHAT HAS BEEN COMPLETED

     Agent A posts content "done" (an ordinary message): room still resolves 200
     and agent B still posts. A participant agent's DELETE /v1/rooms/{slug} → 403;
     only the owner's DELETE → 204, after which GET → 404. So a "done" message
     never ends the room and finishing requires the explicit owner-authorized
     action. NOTE: "finish" today maps to the owner-only soft delete; the distinct
     labeled Finished/archived state (readable-at-URL, reopen, expiry vs delete)
     is task 41's scope — this task only pins the negative guarantee + owner gate.
     PASS (0.45s).

     step 5. Agent A joins, posts msg id1; B posts id2 while A is away; A
     re-handshakes with the SAME identity → still exactly one membership row, role
     "member" preserved. Resuming GET /r/{slug}/messages?after=id1 returns only
     id2 (no repeated work). PASS (0.41s).

      ListFiltered with defaults (single query path; the old List test still holds).
      ListFiltered always excludes private, soft-deleted, expired
      (expires_at IS NULL OR > NOW()), and empty-abandoned rooms
      (message_count=0 AND last_active_at < NOW()-INTERVAL '1 hour' AND no live
      presence). Archived (archived_at) hidden by default, surfaced when a search

      live_agent_count DESC, last_active_at DESC, id DESC; sort=recent (default) =>

      LEFT(content,200) of the latest non-deleted message.

WHY I STOPPED (design conflict, not environment):
  Steps 1, 3, 4, 5 of this task are ALREADY satisfied by existing code:
    [REAL] Agent-created rooms get an atomic room_members 'owner' row for the
      creating agent — db/rooms.go Create() lines 118-130 (INSERT ... role='owner'
      inside the same WithTx as the room INSERT; rolls back together).
    [REAL] Owner agent can manage (update/delete/rotate/archive) via
      handlers/rooms.go canManage() -> memberRepo.IsOwner() (lines 435-453).
    [REAL] A normal 'member' executor is refused owner-only ops (IsOwner=false).
    [REAL] Claiming preserves the room and backfills human ownership —
      agents_claim.go:213-220 -> RoomRepository.BackfillOwnerFromMembership
      (db/rooms.go:430+); room/messages/URL untouched.
  The ONLY unmet requirement is step 2's hard clause:
    "in the final schema room_members is the authoritative owner source and
     rooms.owner_id has been retired after migration."
  Retiring rooms.owner_id is BLOCKED because it has no specified replacement for
  HUMAN room ownership, and dropping it would break passing behavior + tests:

  1. Humans are NEVER represented in room_members. Migration
     000076_create_room_members.up.sql comment: "Humans do NOT get rows here;
     a human's access to a closed room comes from rooms.owner_id (the room owner)
     or the admin role." room_members.agent_id is VARCHAR(50) REFERENCES
     agents(id) — a human user UUID cannot be inserted.
  2. Human-created rooms get NO room_members owner row. db/rooms.go Create()
     inserts the owner membership ONLY when params.CreatorAgentID != "" (line 120);
     a human creating via POST /v1/rooms (JWT) sets owner_id=human and
     CreatorAgentID="". So dropping owner_id would leave EVERY human-created room
     ownerless — breaking the already-passing task "Retain a fast direct-create
     option for authenticated humans".
  3. owner_id (human) is load-bearing across passing paths that have no
     room_members equivalent:
       - middleware/room_access_guard.go:118 — human owner read access to a
         closed room ("if room.OwnerID != nil && claims.UserID == owner").
       - db/rooms.go:345 ListByOwner — the "My rooms" discovery filter
         (handlers/rooms_discovery.go GET my-rooms).
       - models/room_ownership.go SameHumanAsOwner — the DB-free family-access
         source of truth (uses room.OwnerID); shared by the closed-room read
         guard, handshake family clauses, and handlers canManageRoom.
       - agents_claim.go BackfillOwnerFromMembership — sets owner_id on claim.
  4. Intentional, documented design says owner_id STAYS, with no retirement plan:
       - .planning/phases/13-database-foundation/13-CONTEXT.md D-04:
         "owner_id nullable with ON DELETE SET NULL — rooms survive owner
         deletion, needed for Phase 15 data migration."
       - D-31 ports idx_rooms_owner_id; D-42 keeps NULL owner_id for anonymous
         Quorum rooms. No .planning doc mentions retiring owner_id or making
         room_members the authoritative owner source. (grep of .planning for
         "retire"/"authoritative owner" => nothing.)
  5. Blast radius / test breakage (forbidden to weaken existing tests):
       - Room owner_id read/scan/derive sites (non-test): rooms.go handler
         (isRoomOwner, CreateRoom), db/rooms.go (Create insert + 3 scan sites +
         ListByOwner + BackfillOwnerFromMembership), room_access_guard.go:118,
         room_ownership.go, rooms_discovery.go.
       - Tests pinning room owner_id behavior include:
         db/rooms_test.go, db/rooms_backfill_test.go, db/rooms_listfiltered_test.go,
         api/handlers/rooms_test.go, api/router_connect_test.go,
         api/router_homepage_example_test.go, api/router_homepage_overview_test.go
         (plus non-room owner_id in pins/badges/upload/checkpoints tests).
         Dropping the column requires deleting/rewriting these — not allowed
         without a specified replacement that preserves the same behavior.

WHAT A HUMAN MUST DECIDE (the missing design):
  How does HUMAN room ownership survive owner_id retirement? Options a human
  should pick between (do NOT guess — each is a real architecture change):
    (A) Generalize room_members to hold human principals too (nullable agent_id +
        new human_id/user_id column + CHECK exactly-one; rewrite IsOwner,
        ListByOwner, access guard, backfill, handshake family clauses). Large,
        touches private-room admission + tokens + presence.
    (B) Keep a human-owner reference on rooms under a new name and only "retire"
        the users-FK column named owner_id (semantic, not behavioral, retirement).
    (C) Confirm the agent-first product now DROPS human room ownership entirely
        (breaks task 26 "direct-create for authenticated humans" — needs that
        task revisited first).
  Until that decision exists, retiring owner_id would break passing tests and
  invent unspecified architecture — both forbidden by the golden rules.

    "public-to-private changes invalidate ... stream access" = enforce visibility

    and revocation reliable across streams" task (false) + cache invalidation.

FACTS VERIFIED FIRST (read the real code + fetched the spec; no memory answers)
  [REAL] backend/internal/api/router_rooms.go: two namespaces —
    /v1/rooms/* (Solvr JWT / agent key; REST CRUD + public reads + browser SSE)
    and /r/{slug}/* (per-room bearer token; the agent transport). /r/{slug}
    endpoints: POST /message (60/min), GET /messages, GET /messages/{id},
    GET /pins, POST/DELETE /messages/{id}/pin, POST /join, /heartbeat, /leave,
    GET /agents, GET /agents/{agent_name}, POST /claim + /renew + /release,
    GET /claims, POST /events, GET /events, GET /stream (SSE). Plain HTTP+JSON —
    NOT JSON-RPC / gRPC.
  [REAL] backend/internal/hub/registry.go imports
    github.com/a2aproject/a2a-go/a2a and stores *a2a.AgentCard. So Solvr REUSES
    the standard A2A Agent Card SCHEMA. publicCard() strips to Name/Description/
    Skills; URL/Capabilities/SecuritySchemes omitted from public listings.
    ExtendedCard() returns the full card (bearer-verified). Card is supplied at
    join (joinRoomRequest.Card json.RawMessage) and read via
    GET /r/{slug}/agents/{agent_name} (GetAgentCard, rooms_presence.go:210).
    NO /.well-known agent-card discovery; NO standard GetExtendedAgentCard method.
  [REAL] postMessageRequest (rooms_messages.go:75): agent_name, content,
    content_type?, reply_to_entry_id?, client_entry_id? — used verbatim in the
    doc's curl example.
  [REAL] WebFetch a2a-protocol.org/latest/specification: official A2A spec
    current version 1.0.0; bindings JSON-RPC 2.0 / gRPC / HTTP+JSON REST; 11
    standard operations incl. SendMessage(message/send), SendStreamingMessage
    (message/stream), GetTask/ListTasks/CancelTask/SubscribeToTask (tasks/*),
    the 4 push-notification-config ops, and GetExtendedAgentCard.
  [REAL] The only frontend "jsonrpc" claim (api-endpoint-data-core.ts:832) is the
    MCP endpoint block — MCP legitimately IS JSON-RPC 2.0. NOT a false A2A claim;
    left untouched. Product prose ("A2A protocol", "agent-to-agent") is already
    plain-language and never claims standard A2A conformance; left untouched to
    avoid scope creep and test churn on components owned by other tasks.

  NEW frontend/app/docs/protocol/page.tsx (415 lines, server component + SEO
    metadata). Sections satisfy each step:
    - "What Solvr is, and what it is not": states plainly Solvr is NOT a

      protocol detail lives in Docs (steps 3, 4).
    - "Compared with the A2A specification": records spec version 1.0.0, standard

    - "Two route namespaces" + "Room transport endpoints" table (step 2).
    - "Executable examples": real curl for join / message / stream / agent-card,

    - "The Agent Card": supported fields + public-listing strip + no /.well-known

    - "Unsupported standard A2A operations": explicit list — JSON-RPC/gRPC,

WHAT THE FUNNEL DOES (contract in backend/internal/models/funnel_event.go):
  Seven steps across two channels stitched by a non-secret flow_id issued by
  GET /v1/connect. BROWSER (self-reported, POST /v1/analytics/funnel):
  connection_started, starter_prompt_copied, room_viewed, join_prompt_copied.
  SERVER (recorded from confirmed actions, a client can't fake or suppress them):
  room_created, participant_joined (per-actor 1-based ordinal), first_two_way_
  exchange. Stored row keeps flow_id, event, channel, actor_type, PSEUDONYMOUS
  actor_ref (sha256 hash, never a raw id), opaque room_id UUID, preset, role,
  ordinal, entry_surface, instruction_version — NEVER a credential, message body,
  task text or private room title. Milestones dedupe via partial unique indexes +
  ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING so retries/reconnects/replays can't inflate a count.

STEP-BY-STEP COVERAGE (all six task steps map to code + a passing test):
  1 connection_started + starter_prompt_copied(after copy)+flow_id+entry_surface:
    connect-panel.tsx (mount useEffect / post-copy); newFlowID() in connect.go.
    [TEST] connect-panel.funnel.test.tsx 4/4.
  2 flow_id travels in planner prompt + create-room body, persisted server-side:
    connect_prompts.go promptCreateRoomJSON; rooms.go CreateRoom accepts flow_id
    -> RecordRoomCreated. [TEST] router_funnel_test e2e asserts room_created
    carries the exact flow.
  3 room_created / participant_joined(ordinal) / first_two_way_exchange from
    server events, no participant cap: rooms.go, rooms_presence.go (authoritative
    agent id, not agent_name), rooms_messages.go. [TEST] e2e asserts ordinals
    {1,2} and two-way recorded once.
  4 room_viewed + join_prompt_copied(role); agent invites have no browser event
    and none is invented: room-detail-client.tsx, connect-agent-panel.tsx.
    [TEST] room-detail-client.funnel.test + connect-agent-panel.funnel.test; e2e
    proves server steps record with ZERO browser calls.
  5 one documented contract: models.FunnelEventContract() + GET
    /v1/analytics/funnel/contract. [TEST] TestFunnelContract_PublishesVocabulary
    (7 events, right channels, instruction_version).
  6 excludes secrets; server measurement survives blocked browser analytics:
    schema has no body/task/title column; ingest struct accepts none; actor
    pseudonymized. [TEST] TestPseudonymizeActor (never raw id), IngestRecords

WHAT THE MEASUREMENT DOES (contract in internal/db/cohort_comparison.go):
  A new operator-only report, GET /admin/cohort-comparison?launch=<RFC3339>,
  backed by db.CohortComparisonRepository.Compare(ctx, launch, now). One launch
  timestamp anchors two windows, [launch, launch+7d) and [launch, launch+28d),
  both measured in UTC with IDENTICAL definitions so the redesign is judged over
  consistent post-launch cohorts, not a moving all-time bag. Per window:
    - rooms_created and activated_rooms (deduped first_two_way_exchange) reported
      SEPARATELY, plus room_to_activation conversion with an explicit denominator,
      so more room CREATION is never conflated with more successful COLLABORATION.
    - second_agent_connection_failures: rooms where a 2nd agent actually joined
      (participant ordinal >= 2) but no two-way exchange happened -- the connection
      was attempted and failed, distinct from "no 2nd agent ever arrived".
    - time_to_first_exchange_ms as continuous median + p90 (percentile_cont),
      Count/Median/P90 pointers so an empty window reads as no-measurement.
    - rooms_with_return: rooms with a later agent session (participant_joined /
      room_viewed) after the exchange.
    - room_to_post_publications: non-deleted posts with source_room_id in the
      window's created rooms.
    - visibility split (public/private/unknown via rooms.is_private; unknown =
      created room with no resolvable rooms row, never guessed) and creator split
      (new/returning by earliest funnel appearance of the creator ref / unknown
      for a null ref).
  Window.Complete = now >= window_end; a partial window still reports its numbers
  labeled incomplete, never omitted or zero-filled. All-time multi-author rooms
  (message-based, looser) carried as historical_context with a note, never merged
  into a cohort. external_sources.google_analytics / .search_console report
  {available:false, note} -- GA/GSC are not connected as data sources ([REAL]:
  grep found only credential-redaction regex in operator_analytics.go, no data
  pull), so they are marked unavailable, never substituted with zeros.

2026-09-24 — Task idx 68 (spec.json, category data) — CUTOVER DONE, passes=true
"Store room messages and coordination events in one ordered room timeline"
================================================================================
TASK SELECTION: same top-down skips as the previous entry (idx 23/27/51/52/58/66/67,
  reasons unchanged); idx 68 was IN PROGRESS (foundation committed in 04dc3a4), so this
  iteration continued it as the journal instructed.

DESIGN (single storage, zero reader churn):
  migration 000094_room_entries_cutover makes room_entries the ONLY storage:
  - Rebuilds the 000093 backfill so every MESSAGE entry keeps its legacy messages.id
    (identity). Old deep links + SSE Last-Event-ID / ?after= cursors therefore resolve
    unchanged; legacy EVENT ids resolve via room_entry_legacy_map. Safe: nothing but the
    000093 backfill had ever written room_entries (grep: no production caller).
  - Archives the old tables as legacy_messages / legacy_room_events (frozen; used by the
    down migration and the idempotent backfill function room_entries_backfill_legacy()).
    legacy_messages.id default moved onto room_entries_id_seq so no id can collide.
  - `messages` and `room_events` are now VIEWS over room_entries with INSTEAD OF
    INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE triggers: each write = exactly one entry, so every existing
    reader (transcript, event type/issue filters, homepage activity/rooms/overview,
    activation/cohort/funnel analytics, migrate-quorum) derives from the timeline and no
    compatibility path can insert a duplicate.
  - BEFORE INSERT trigger room_entries_allocate: for live writes takes the room row lock
    (FOR NO KEY UPDATE), allocates sequence = MAX+1 AND a fresh id under that lock (so
    id order == sequence order within a room), and enforces same-room reply_to /
    supersedes / addressed_member_ids (constraint name room_entries_same_room_reference).
    Addressed participant = an identity the room already records: room_members.agent_id,
    agent_presence.agent_name, or author_id / display label of one of its message
    entries (union of the existing participant notions in authorizeParticipant and
    ListRoomParticipants; addressing stays a routing hint). Backfill rows carry an
    explicit sequence and skip it; backfill nulls any cross-room legacy reply/supersede
    (0 such rows locally) instead of inventing causality.
  - rooms.result_message_id FK re-pointed to room_entries(id) (ids unchanged).
  - New timeline indexes replacing the legacy ones: room_created, message_id,
    event_recent, room_pinned, supersedes.
  - room_claims and agent_presence untouched (expiring leases / presence stay separate).

FILES (git status --porcelain / git diff --stat, file by file):
  NEW backend/migrations/000094_room_entries_cutover.up.sql (299L) / .down.sql (55L;
      restores real tables AND copies back post-cutover entries + edits).
  M   backend/internal/db/room_entries.go: Create relies on the trigger for sequence/id
      (removed its own FOR UPDATE + MAX subquery); BackfillFromLegacy now calls
      room_entries_backfill_legacy() (the 3 duplicated SQL constants removed); new
      ErrInvalidEntryReference + asInvalidEntryReference mapper.
  M   backend/internal/db/room_messages.go: Create no longer computes sequence_num
      (timeline allocates it); maps same-room violations to ErrInvalidEntryReference.
  M   backend/internal/api/handlers/rooms_messages.go: agent + human post paths return
      400 VALIDATION_ERROR (not 500) on ErrInvalidEntryReference.
  NEW backend/internal/db/room_entries_cutover_test.go (8 integration tests).
  NEW backend/internal/api/handlers/rooms_messages_references_test.go (2 tests).

WHAT THIS SLICE DOES (migration 000095_room_members_authority):
  - room_members: new surrogate PK id (UUID); agent_id now nullable; new user_id
    (users FK, ON DELETE CASCADE), display_role (1..64 chars, optional), revoked_at.
    CHECK num_nonnulls(agent_id,user_id)=1 (exactly one actor). UNIQUE(room_id,agent_id)
    and UNIQUE(room_id,user_id) = one membership row, so at most one active membership,
    per actor and room. The unique constraint on (room_id, agent_id) is deliberately
    NON-partial, so existing ON CONFLICT (room_id, agent_id) callers (RoomRepository.Create,
    router_rooms_acl_test addMember helper) keep working unchanged. created_at = join time.
  - Backfill: one active human 'owner' row per rooms.owner_id. Trigger
    rooms_sync_human_owner mirrors every non-null owner_id (INSERT or UPDATE OF owner_id)
    during the compatibility window, so room creation (same tx) and claim backfill
    (BackfillOwnerFromMembership) both establish the human owner membership.
  - Final-owner guard: deferred constraint trigger room_members_keep_owner refuses to
    revoke, demote or delete a live room's last active owner (check_violation,
    CONSTRAINT room_members_final_owner). Deferred so a transfer can promote and demote in
    any order inside one tx. Allowed when the room is soft- or hard-deleted. When the
    owner's ACCOUNT is hard-deleted (agent or user row gone), the now-ownerless room is
    ARCHIVED (archived_at) instead = the documented terminal state. Agent unlinking
    (human_id -> NULL) touches no membership.
  - Credential binding: trigger room_members_revoke_credentials deletes the agent's
    room_agent_tokens row when its membership is revoked.
  - Repo (internal/db/room_members.go): Remove = UPDATE revoked_at (row kept), NotFound
    when not active; Add readmits (clears revoked_at, fresh created_at) and maps the
    guard to new ErrLastRoomOwner; Get/IsMember/IsOwner/ListByRoom only see active rows
    (ListByRoom agent rows only, so the members API shape is unchanged).
  - Handler: POST/DELETE /v1/rooms/{slug}/members return 409 LAST_OWNER on
    ErrLastRoomOwner (before: demote returned 400 INVALID_AGENT, remove returned 500).

WHAT THIS SLICE DOES
  Migration 000096_room_family_access:
  - room_members.access_source TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'direct' CHECK IN ('direct','family').
    All existing rows backfill to 'direct' (no row was ever family-sourced before).
  - Family access is DERIVED per request, never stored: the agent's CURRENT human_id must be
    a live user (users.deleted_at IS NULL) holding an ACTIVE owner membership in the room.
  - A handshake authorized only by family access materializes the agent's membership with
    access_source='family'. Triggers revoke those rows (and, through 000095's credential
    trigger, delete their solvr_rt_ tokens) when the justification ends:
      agents_end_family_memberships      — agents.human_id changes;
      room_members_end_family_on_owner_loss — the human's owner row is revoked, demoted or
                                             deleted;
      users_end_family_memberships       — users.deleted_at set (DELETE /v1/me soft delete).
  - An explicit owner grant (members API Add) sets access_source='direct' and survives all
    of the above; AddFamily never downgrades an existing active direct/owner membership.
  Readers switched off rooms.owner_id:
  - middleware/room_access_guard.go roomMemberAccessAllowed: agent = active membership OR
    IsFamilyOwner; human = admin OR IsUserMember (was room.OwnerID compare + SameHumanAsOwner).

    (agent). It no longer calls canManageRoom.

    ensureMember uses AddFamily for a family grant.

FILES ([REAL] git status --porcelain: 1 modified, 2 new; git diff --stat: posts.go +13):
  NEW backend/internal/api/handlers/posts_concurrency.go (51 lines):
    postETag(updatedAt) -> strong ETag `"<UnixMicro>"` (UnixMicro matches Postgres
      timestamptz precision, so the validator is byte-stable across a GET->PATCH round trip);
    ifMatchIsStale(r, currentETag) -> true only when an If-Match header is PRESENT and no
      candidate matches (optional header never stale; "*" matches; W/ weak prefix compared
      by value; comma-separated list supported);
    enforceIfMatch(w, r, updatedAt) -> writes 412 PRECONDITION_FAILED + echoes current ETag
      and returns true when stale, else false.
  M   backend/internal/api/handlers/posts.go (+13 lines, 957 -> 970):
    Get: sets ETag: postETag(post.UpdatedAt) before the 200 body.
    Update: after the ownership 403 check (so a non-owner never learns the version), calls
      `if enforceIfMatch(w, r, existingPost.UpdatedAt) { return }`; on success echoes
      ETag: postETag(result.UpdatedAt) so the client's next If-Match is current.
  NEW backend/internal/api/handlers/posts_concurrency_test.go (205 lines):
    6 handler tests (mock repo, NO DB => they RUN, not SKIP): stale If-Match=>412 &
    not-persisted & current-ETag echoed; matching If-Match=>200 persists; "*"=>200;
    no-If-Match=>200 (back-compat); non-owner+stale=>403 (ownership before precondition);
    GET sets ETag. Plus TestIfMatchIsStale: 8-case table pinning the parse contract.
  NO existing test deleted, skipped, renamed or weakened — all 15 existing TestUpdatePost_*
    still pass. Design is non-breaking: If-Match is OPT-IN (absent header keeps prior behavior).
